`ums` on the board: the gadget binds and the command runs; the host saw nothing (tasks 3.2, 3.3)
=================================================================================================
2026-09-22, 08:44-08:53, solomon. The card carries the ums stage 1
(/nix/store/8jpkdpxmhyysafqv5gk8vcimw347ikqq-k230-sd-image.img, docs/evidence/uboot-ums-build.txt);
the coordinator wrote it and confirmed from the board that the 2 MiB slot hashes to bd562f2b. Two
sessions, both driven by tools/ums-session.py under /tmp/k230-board.lock, both ending with the board
back at its Linux login prompt before the lock was released. The transcripts are below, verbatim.

Session 1 ended early by the tool's own fault: it matched the previous command's `K230# ` still in
its buffer and treated `ums` as having returned 100 ms after starting it. Fixed in the tool (the
match buffer is now cleared on every send) and re-run as session 2. Session 1 is kept because
`dm tree` and the first 100 ms of `ums` are already evidence.

What was observed
-----------------
3.2  `dm tree`, the after half of design D3 (both sessions, identical):

       usb           0  [   ]   dwc2-udc-otg          |   |-- usb-otg@91500000
       usb           1  [   ]   dwc2-udc-otg          |   |-- usb-otg@91540000

     Before (docs/evidence/uboot-ums-hardware.txt): one node, usb-otg@91540000, bound to the host
     driver dwc2_usb; usb-otg@91500000 absent. After: usb-otg@91500000 exists (the device-tree
     patch) and BOTH nodes are bound to the gadget driver dwc2-udc-otg -- usbotg1 included, whose
     dr_mode is still "otg". With CONFIG_USB_DWC2 off there is no host driver for the gadget to
     beat, so this does not by itself prove the linker-list ordering claim; it does prove the half
     that matters for the follow-up: the gadget driver binds any snps,dwc2 node it is offered, so
     keeping USB host (task 6.1) needs the .bind that refuses dr_mode = "host", exactly as D3 says.

3.3  `ums 0 mmc 1` on the board:

       UMS: LUN 0, dev mmc 1, hwpart 0, sector 0x0, count 0xee4c000

     0xee4c000 sectors = 249 872 384 x 512 = 127 931 060 224 bytes = 119.1 GiB, the card `mmc info`
     reported in the baseline. The spinner ran until Ctrl-C (`CTRL+C - Operation aborted`) and the
     prompt came back; no "g_dnl_register failed", no "No USB device controller". So the UDC
     probed, the mass-storage function registered, and U-Boot was serving the card for the whole
     of session 2's 300 s.

     On the host, in those 300 s: no new `lsusb` line, no new /dev/disk/by-id/usb-* disk, polled
     every 0.5 s against a snapshot taken before the command (the reader on sde/sdf and the CH342
     console 1a86:55d2 are in the snapshot and were excluded that way, not by name). NOT
     ENUMERATED. Task 3.3 is not grounded; neither are 3.4 and 3.5, which need the block device.

     Two candidate reasons, undecidable from the console. (a) The J3 cable: the coordinator said
     it was being connected as the session started and might land a minute or two in; this file
     cannot say whether it was in, or in J3 rather than J2, during the window. (b) dwc2 B-session /
     VBUS detection, the risk design.md named: if the controller never sees a valid B-session it
     never pulls D+ and the host sees nothing, and the fix is a device-tree line --
     `u-boot,force-b-session-valid` and/or `u-boot,force-vbus-detection`, both read by
     dwc2_udc_otg_of_to_plat() in this tree. (a) must be settled before (b) is tried: a rebuild
     for (b) costs a card write.

=====================================================================================================
Sessions 3 and 4, 08:58-09:08: a different cable, then the core's own registers (task 3.3, continued)
=====================================================================================================
The user swapped the J3 cable and said "check now". Session 3 re-ran tools/ums-session.py on the
SAME firmware (nothing rebuilt, nothing written), ums held for 240 s: no new lsusb entry, no new
by-id disk. Session 4 added a read-only register dump of usbotg0 (md.l at 0x91500000 and
0x91500800) before `ums` and again right after Ctrl-C -- the same reads the driver itself makes
at probe; no clocks are managed on this SoC (dwc2_udc_otg_clk_init: clk_get_bulk returns -ENOENT
and the driver carries on) -- ums held for 90 s: again nothing on the host.

The registers (session 4; bit names from drivers/usb/gadget/dwc2_udc_otg_regs.h in the tree)
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
                 before ums   after Ctrl-C
  GOTGCTL  0x00  0x000d0000   0x000d0000   bit19 B_SESSION_VALID (regs.h:91) = 1, bit18 A_SESSION_VALID (:92) = 1,
                                           bit16 ConIDSts = 1 (B-device). THE PHY SEES VBUS FROM THE CABLE, both times.
  GUSBCFG  0x0c  0x10001400   0x0000540f   after: turnaround 5, 16-bit UTMI+ (bit3), FS timeout 7 -- reconfig_usbd()'s
                                           dflt_gusbcfg (dwc2_udc_otg.c:468-483); no FDMOD, so mode comes from ID.
  GINTSTS  0x14  0x54000028   0x800c3804   after: bit31 INT_RESUME, 19 INT_OUT_EP, 18 INT_IN_EP, 13 INT_ENUMDONE,
                                           12 INT_RESET, 11 INT_SUSPEND, 2 INT_OTG (regs.h:123-135); bit0 = 0, device mode.
                                           A USB RESET WAS SEEN ON THE BUS AND SPEED ENUMERATION COMPLETED, then suspend.
  DCTL     0x804 0x00000002   0x00000000   bit1 SOFT_DISCONNECT (regs.h:172): set before, CLEAR after -- the driver pulled D+ up.
  DSTS     0x808 0x0007ff02   0x00c00002   bits[2:1] EnumSpd = 01: FULL speed, on a high-speed core with a 16-bit PHY.

So on the board's side the gadget did everything a gadget does up to the point where a host asks
for descriptors: VBUS valid, D+ pulled up, a bus reset received, speed enumerated (at full speed,
which is itself odd for this core and worth remembering), then the bus went quiet (suspend).

The host's side
---------------
solomon's kernel log for the whole window 08:44-09:09 -- unfiltered, every line -- is one line:

  Sep 22 08:58:02 solomon kernel: usb 4-4: USB disconnect, device number 8      (the card reader being unplugged)

and USB attach logging on this host demonstrably works (the console bridge's own re-plugs are
logged as `usb 3-1: new full-speed USB device number 59 using xhci_hcd` at 08:39:44). A device
that attached to any port of this machine and then failed enumeration would have produced
`new full-speed USB device` followed by descriptor errors. Nothing did.

What this means
---------------
Something on the far end of the J3 cable acted as a USB host -- it issued a bus reset -- and it
was not this machine. Cable/port, not firmware: the design's named risk (dwc2 never seeing a valid
B-session, to be fixed with `u-boot,force-b-session-valid`) is ruled out by GOTGCTL bit 19, and
that mitigation would have been inert here anyway: dwc2_udc_otg_of_to_plat() parses both
u-boot,force-* properties (dwc2_udc_otg.c:1014-1018) but dwc2_udc_otg_probe() only acts on them
inside `if (plat->activate_stm_id_vb_detection)` (:1121-1158), a flag set only by
dwc2_set_stm32mp1_hsotg_params() (:1030-1043), which only the st,stm32mp15-hsotg compatible
reaches through its driver data. For snps,dwc2 they do nothing. No VBUS-forcing variant was
built.

Next: confirm the far end of the J3 cable is plugged into THIS host (solomon), and into which
port; then re-run tools/ums-session.py on the same firmware with `journalctl -k -f` watching.
Nothing about the firmware should change until that has been done once.

=====================================================================================================
Session 5, 09:13-09:15: the card enumerates on this host over ums (task 3.3 grounded; 3.4 in part)
=====================================================================================================
The cable's far end was proven to reach this machine first: the user plugged a phone into it and
solomon logged `usb 3-4: new high-speed USB device number 64 ... Product: Pixel 10 Pro` at 09:12:24
(in the kernel-log excerpt below). The same cable was then moved to the board's J3. Same firmware as
sessions 1-4 (2 MiB slot bd562f2b), nothing rebuilt, nothing written. tools/ums-session.py, --regs.

What the host saw, 5 s after `ums 0 mmc 1`
------------------------------------------
  kernel:  usb 3-4: new high-speed USB device number 65 using xhci_hcd
           usb 3-4: New USB device found, idVendor=29f1, idProduct=0230, bcdDevice= 2.27
           usb 3-4: Product: USB download gadget      Manufacturer: U-Boot
           usb-storage 3-4:1.0: USB Mass Storage device detected
           scsi 8:0:0:0: Direct-Access     Linux    UMS disk 0       ffff PQ: 0 ANSI: 2
           sd 8:0:0:0: [sde] Attached SCSI removable disk
  lsusb:   ID 29f1:0230 Canaan Creative Co., Ltd USB download gadget
  sysfs:   3-4: 29f1:0230 speed=480 Mb/s version= 2.00 manufacturer=U-Boot product=USB download gadget
  by-id:   /dev/disk/by-id/usb-Linux_UMS_disk_0-0:0 -> /dev/sde   (+ -part1, -part2)
  size:    /sys/class/block/sde/size = 249872384 sectors; lsblk -b = 127934660608 bytes = 119.1 GiB
           -- exactly `mmc info`'s card (docs/evidence/uboot-ums-hardware.txt) and exactly
           `UMS: ... count 0xee4c000` (0xee4c000 = 249872384).
  lsblk:   sde1  112M  ext4  K230_BOOT     sde2  1.9G  ext4  NIXOS_SD     ID_PART_TABLE_TYPE=dos
           (the layout nix/sd-image.nix writes: boot at 4 MiB, root at 128 MiB)
  mounted: nothing -- no automounter touched it.
  hash:    sha256 of 350046 bytes at offset 2 MiB of /dev/sde, read over USB:
             bd562f2bcff46ba9352061b08074f19df2f17dd0ca080293b47c20e3f5a6e50e
           = result-stage1/fn_ug_u-boot.bin of the ums build (docs/evidence/uboot-ums-build.txt)
           = what the board hashed from its own mmcblk1 (coordinator, task 3.1).
           The read path through ums returns the card's bytes.

The core's registers, this time
-------------------------------
  before ums:  GOTGCTL 0x000d0000 (B_SESSION_VALID), DCTL 0x00000002 (soft-disconnect), as before.
  after Ctrl-C: GINTSTS 0x800c3804 (RESET, ENUMDONE, SUSPEND seen); DSTS 0x002d5000 -- EnumSpd
               bits[2:1] = 00 = HIGH speed. Sessions 3-4, on the other cable, showed EnumSpd 01 =
               full speed: whatever was on that cable's far end negotiated at 12 Mb/s and was not
               this machine; this host negotiated 480 Mb/s. The full-speed oddity was the other
               host's, not the board's.
  The by-id node was still present 10 s after Ctrl-C: U-Boot's `ums` stops serving on Ctrl-C but
  does not drop the pull-up until the controller is reinitialised; the host noticed the disconnect
  when the board rebooted into Linux. Harmless; noted so the next reader does not chase it.

What is grounded, and what is not
---------------------------------
  3.3  Grounded: a by-id path exists whose size matches 1.4, console and host output are here.
  3.4  Partly: 350 046 bytes of the 2.2 GB image compared, at the one offset that identifies the
       stage 1. The task's own check -- cmp of the whole image against the device -- was not run in
       this session (it is read-only and needs no authorisation; it needs a few minutes of the
       board, and the instruction for this session was to report and stop).
  3.5  Needs the first write over ums, which the coordinator authorises separately.
